What IS established is that the encoding **corrupts**: the sweep's own `while IFS='|' read` mis-parses the same rows, so the gate would run against garbled paths and print blank diff lines, and the first draft's "prevents drift" claim is false in exactly that case. Adopted on *that* ground, at zero cost (`find -print0` already emits NUL), and **recorded with the correction rather than laundered into agreement** — the generalisation worth more than the instance: rules 3a/3b/3d police claims flowing *down* and 3f polices a reviewer's *premise*; none of them covers an objection whose defect is real and whose stated mechanism is wrong, which is the shape most likely to be waved through *because* the fix is obviously good. **AN INSTRUMENT OF MY OWN FAILED, AND ITS OUTPUT WAS DISCARDED RATHER THAN BANKED.** My first newline probe printed `>>> COMPARE PASSES — PIN DEFEATED` — the exact direction I was predicting, i.e. rule 3d in its purest form. The file had never been created (the path needed a nonexistent intermediate directory), so a 1-file tree matched a 1-entry allowlist **vacuously**. Only an asserted creation-count caught it; the re-run with `[ "$n" -eq 2 ] || abort` is what makes V25/V26 evidence. A control that ABORTS is worth more than one that merely prints. **GATES:** baseline `./scripts/verify_ail.sh` **rc=0** on a pristine tree, `4/11/14` UNMOVED, Leg 3 `9/9 steps performed non-zero work`; every mutation restored and `git status --porcelain` re-verified empty after each (storejournal sha `adf0760b…5079` held). Local `go` is **1.26.4**, so `verify_go.sh` needs `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.6` — re-confirmed as a BASE condition, not a regression. **RULED OUT:** an `EXACT_TOTAL_MODULES` count literal (defeated by the composed mutant, and a third literal for item 13 to maintain); pinning `world/` only (the sketch-deletion arm is half the measured defect); sweeping `packages/world-core/*.ail` (already pinned by identity AND by SHA-256 in Leg 3 steps 2–3) or `host/replay/testdata/transition_fixture.ail` (a Go replay input, gated by `host/replay`'s tests); leaving the arms on the live tree; citing `host/broker`'s AST gate as the collision; treating gpt5's exploit as reproduced; and running the sprint this iteration (the doc-less head's routing step is designer→quorum, and a planner+executor+judge+CI chain would not have fitted the remaining slot — attempting it is how an iteration dies mid-flight holding unlanded work). **ZERO OPEN ASKS. NEXT:** the item-12 **sprint** — `sprint-planner` on this doc, then execute (~0.5d, top of band; the doc names its own fallback if the planner prices the Leg-1 loop restructure higher). Then item **16** (`host/broker` ~18% base flake). Item 5 `P6.B` remains UNBLOCKED.

That is the ordering argument, and it is stronger than the concurrency-window argument iter-55 recorded. **A HYPOTHESIS OF MINE WAS REFUTED EN ROUTE AND THE REFUTATION IS THE FINDING**: I predicted the residue would leave a NON-COMPILING file and brick the build. It does not — the gate's own inline comment already says *"compiling HTTP import"*. So the charter queue row, the iter-55 stamp and the dashboard were all wrong, three times over, to call the mutants *"deliberately non-compiling"*, and iter-55's transient `could not import net/http/httputil` CI red therefore **cannot** be explained by "the file is broken"; it is a build-graph artifact of the file *changing* under a concurrent build. Corrected in all three places this Gate 4. **DECISION**: `go list -overlay` + an overlay-aware read, so the gate never writes a byte under the repo root — closing crash-residue and the concurrency window with ONE mechanism. **Controller-verified rather than inherited**: the overlay closure is **diff-IDENTICAL** to a physically poisoned tree (control fired — baseline vs poisoned differ by **69** packages) and the repo tree stayed **0-dirty** throughout; the queue row's preferred scratch-copy repair was rejected because `repoRoot` bakes the real root in at **compile** time (`runtime.Caller(0)`) — a constraint I proved accidentally when a stale test binary from a deleted worktree made 8 timeout runs fail before reaching any mutation, i.e. eight clean-looking "no residue" results from an instrument that never ran. **TWO QUORUM ROUNDS, BOTH BLOCKED, BOTH ACCEPTED RATHER THAN ARGUED.** Round 1 — both reviewers, ONE objection: AC1/M3's ~2 ms polling detector was probabilistic, so a write-and-restore between ticks passed silently. They were right, and the irony is the spine: a detector whose green means *zero observations* is this item's own defect class. **Deleted, not supplemented**, and replaced by BOTH fixes as complementary layers — `gpt5-6-sol`'s structural write confinement (a writer rejecting any `repoRoot` destination) + a real `go/parser`+`go/ast` guard as the filesystem-independent PRIMARY, and `gemini-3-1-pro`'s `ModTime` check as a deterministic backstop. **Per rule 3f the controller MEASURED gemini's premise instead of forwarding it**: back-to-back write+restore advanced `st_mtime_ns` in **200/200** trials and **200/200** with a 1 ms gap, against a firing KP pair (no-write → unchanged `True`; single-write → changed 200/200) — so the backstop is deterministic, and the ext4 caveat is stated rather than assumed (measured on APFS only). Round 2 — TWO NEW objections, resolved under the **narrow-refinement carve-out** (precedent: iter-51 round 3); **neither disputed the design DIRECTION**, both carried a concrete reviewer-authored `proposed_fix`, so the applied text is the reviewers' **verbatim** remedy: (a) `gpt5-6-sol` — `AC4/M5`'s *"≥10 randomized offsets"* never established that any kill landed **after** an arm armed its overlay, so a clean tree could mean the threat was never exercised; replaced by its bounded barrier protocol (arm-specific ready marker, artifact verification, then SIGKILL, with a missing marker/timeout/absent artifact/early exit **failing** the criterion, one run per arm). (b) `gemini-3-1-pro` — **P9's audit had not searched for its own threat model**: `V15` grepped `os.WriteFile` alone while the design's Decision 8 names `os.OpenFile`/`os.Create`/`os.Rename` as mutators. **Gemini was right and the controller measured it two ways**: its widened search verbatim → **25** hits across 13 test files with `host/boundary` as the firing KP (**3**), the only `Create/OpenFile/Rename` outside `host/boundary` being `cli_test.go:272`, `t.TempDir()`-rooted; and then the property itself, measured end-to-end rather than read off 25 call sites — sampling `git status --porcelain` at 20 Hz across the WHOLE suite gives **1** distinct dirty observation with the gate enabled (` M cmd/ailang-worldd/main.go` — the sampler fires) and **0** with only `TestWorldBoundaryDependencyAllowlist` skipped. **P9 is now a measurement, not a search.** Both arms also exited `rc=1` on the SAME pre-existing cause and it was chased rather than waved through: `TestEpisodeLiveReplayThreeArmsAndEvidence` → *"AILANG_BIN must name the pinned released interpreter"*, which is `verify_go.sh`'s deliberate fail-loud guard firing at a bare `go test` that bypassed the script — identical in both arms, so the comparison is unaffected, and CI is green at `deeb804`. **A SECOND, PRE-EXISTING FINDING, CONTROLLER-CONFIRMED AND CARRIED AS `10/OD-1` RATHER THAN FIXED**: `checkGoGroup` (`:130`) computes the `go list -deps` closure and then uses it **only** as a `len(deps)==0` anti-vacuity guard — the forbidden-prefix scan walks **direct import specs alone**, so a TRANSITIVE forbidden dependency is invisible in a gate named *"dependency allowlist"*. **INSTRUMENT DISCIPLINE, INCLUDING MY OWN FAILURES:** my first SIGKILL sampler recomputed baselines via `git show` per round and lost the race to the restore — that result was **UNINFORMATIVE, not negative**, and was re-run with precomputed baselines before anything was believed; my package counts (165→234) were taken under the PATH **go1.26.4**, which `verify_go.sh:76` **explicitly denies**, so the doc's `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.6` numbers (160→229) are the operative ones and the equivalence result merely now holds on two toolchains; and my `awk` table-integrity check reported 10 malformed rows by counting escaped `\|` inside code spans — 0 malformed on a correct count. ## STATUS 2026-08-06 (iteration 55) — **`AC12` REPAIRED (PR #45 → squash `1761a9c`; dev CI green BOTH jobs, SHA-addressed on the merge commit) — THE CARRY-FORWARD THE LAST TWO ITERATIONS WROTE DOWN WAS DISCHARGED EARLY, AND DISCHARGING IT EARLY IS WHAT FOUND THE HOLE.** **THE SPINE: AN EXCEPTION IS A CLAIM ABOUT ONE THING, AND THE CHEAPEST WAY TO LOSE A GUARD IS TO EXPRESS THAT EXCEPTION AS AN OMISSION FROM A SHARED LIST.** Iterations 53 and 54 both recorded, in as many words, that `AC12`'s *"network confined to `host/broker`"* control is **VACUOUS until SM.B2a** and must be re-asserted there rather than inherited as green. This iteration re-asserted it **at the boundary, before the network code exists** — and the gate turned out to be weaker than vacuous in a second way nobody had recorded. `host/boundary/allowlist_world_test.go` protects three Go groups, and its loopback exception is true of **exactly one** of them: `cmd/ailang-worldd` genuinely needs `net/http` for its loopback-only daemon client, and its own code comment at `:31-33` says so. But the exception was expressed by **omitting bare `net/http` from the single global `forbiddenImportPrefixes`**, so `host/store` and `host/replay` silently inherited it. **Measured first-party, every mutation confirmed landed by sha256 BEFORE the result was believed, every restore verified byte-identical**: bare `net/http` blank-imported into `host/store/store.go` (`40315426…`→`53b45ebd…`) left the gate **rc=0 PASS**; into `host/replay/replay.go` likewise **rc=0 PASS**; while the `net/http/httputil` arm correctly **rc=1 RED**, naming the exact path — so the instrument worked and the threat still walked through it. **THE REASON IT SURVIVED IS ITERATION 54'S OWN SPINE, ARRIVING INSIDE THE GATE ITERATION 53 LANDED**: every protected group's `mutantImport` was `net/http/httputil`, so the gate had only ever been tested against **a mutation shaped to the check rather than to the threat**. **AND THE EXEMPTION WAS UNFORCED, NOT MERELY UNNECESSARY — the alternative explanation was measured and REFUTED (rule 3d) rather than assumed**: the natural defence is *"bare `net/http` must already be in those closures, so forbidding it would red the baseline."* Baseline `net/http` presence per dependency closure is `host/store` **0** (160 deps), `host/replay` **0** (162), `cmd/ailang-worldd` **1** (233), control `host/hashref` **0**. Only one group ever needed the exception; the other two could always have been held to the stricter rule. Repaired with a per-group `extraForbidden` field — the exception now lives where it is true — plus `TestBareNetHTTPExemptionIsPerGroup`, which pins the asymmetry so collapsing it back into one global list **fails loudly** instead of silently re-granting the exemption; proven non-vacuous by setting `host/store`'s entry to `nil` → **RED naming the group** (`forbiddenImport("net/http") for host/store = false, want true`), restore byte-identical. Post-repair, all five arms: both threats **RED**, `httputil` still **RED**, `cmd/ailang-worldd` still **PASS**, pristine **green**. **WHAT SM.B2a STILL MUST RE-ASSERT, AND MUST NOT INHERIT:** the *positive* half is still unproven — that network code, once it EXISTS in `host/broker`, is genuinely permitted there. `host/broker` has **zero** `net/http` deps at HEAD (control: 8 files repo-wide carry it), and the gate's green control asserts only that the broker's dependency closure is **NON-EMPTY** (`:281`), which is true of every Go package alive. **THE SECOND FINDING IS PRE-EXISTING, WAS NOT IN THE PICK, AND IS NOW QUEUE ITEM 10.** The same gate proves its teeth by **rewriting three OTHER packages' production source files in the live working tree** (`mutateAndRestore` at `:271` → `cmd/ailang-worldd/main.go`, `host/store/store.go`, `host/replay/replay.go`, each with a deliberately non-compiling import) while `go test ./...` builds those packages **concurrently**. ## STATUS 2026-08-05 (iteration 54) — **`w-self-mod-vertical` MILESTONE `SM.B1` LANDED (PR #43 → squash `1856bfb`; dev CI green BOTH jobs, SHA-addressed on the merge commit) — AND BOTH OF THIS ITERATION'S FINDINGS ARE THE SAME DEFECT WEARING DIFFERENT CLOTHES: A CHECK THAT WAS NEVER POINTED AT THE THING IT GUARDS.** **THE SPINE: A MUTATION SHAPED TO THE CHECK TESTS THE CHECK, NOT THE THREAT.** `SM.B1`'s `AC-B1.2` is the milestone's own declared gating criterion — `schemaV2SQL` must be AUTHORED, never derived from `schemaSQL`, or the ledger compares the schema to itself and the gate is a no-op. `TestSchemaVersionLedgerIsIndependent` greps **its own source file**; its two **negative** needles were carefully split (`"var schemaV2SQL = "+"schemaSQL"`) so they would not match the very lines containing them, while its **positive** needle was one literal, `"const schemaV2SQL = schemaV1SQL +"`, which **the check's own line satisfies** — so it passed no matter what the declaration said. The asymmetry IS the lesson: the split was applied exactly where omitting it makes a check fire **ALWAYS** (immediate, unmissable symptom) and omitted where it makes a check fire **NEVER** (no symptom at all). Measured, mutation confirmed landed by sha256 before the result was believed: `var schemaV2SQL = string(schemaSQL)` — the ledger becoming the very file it exists to independently attest — left the test reporting **`ok 0.290s`**, both negative needles dodged by the `string(...)` conversion, the DDL comparison then comparing `schema.sql` against itself. **The executor's own `MUT-SM-V2-LEDGER-DERIVED` DID red, which is precisely why this survived it**: it used the bare `var schemaV2SQL = schemaSQL` form its negative needle was written to catch. Repaired with the instrument this charter's OWN rotation already uses — **anchor to `^`**, where an indented check-line cannot reach — plus a semantic backstop (`schemaV2SQL == schemaSQL` is a hard failure) immune to every needle game; post-repair **both** mutation forms RED, control green, restore byte-identical `76d63695…`. **THE SECOND FINDING WAS NOT IN THE PICK AT ALL — IT WAS IN THE PREVIOUS ITERATION'S LANDING.** The Gate-2 reality check listed `13315da`'s files and one was **`ailang-worldd`: a 15,740,242-byte darwin/arm64 Mach-O**, `A`-added by SM.A, tracked at HEAD in a 142-file repo, referenced by nothing (every in-tree mention is `cmd/ailang-worldd`, the *source package*; `cli_test.go:125` builds to a temp path; **no script or workflow builds to the repo root**, so it was hand-swept, not loop-produced). Stale-base tell in this charter's own lowercase spelling (`iteration 53` → **1**) **with its known-present control** (`iteration 52` → **1**); structural invariant `^## STATUS 2026` → **3** pre-rotation, **3** post; rotation arithmetic asserted before writing (`after == before + 2 − 2×1`). 4 stamps → newest 3 kept (iter-54, iter-53, iter-52); the positionally-4th (iter-51) archived this Gate 4. **AND THEN DEV WENT RED ON THIS ITERATION'S OWN DOCS-ONLY BOOKKEEPING COMMIT — A THIRD INSTANCE OF THE SAME FAMILY, THIS TIME IN THE INSTRUMENT'S PLUMBING RATHER THAN ITS LOGIC.** `ailang-code verify gate` failed on `a6cc473` with exit **141** (= 128+13, **SIGPIPE**) in SM.A's own *Install PINNED v0.30.0 ailang for the world-package leg only* step. A docs-only commit, so the red had no cause in its own diff. Mechanism: `"$BIN" --version | grep -q 'AILANG v0.30.0'` under `set -o pipefail` — `grep -q` exits at its FIRST match while `--version` still has **6 of its 7 lines** to write, the writer takes SIGPIPE, and `pipefail` promotes that to the step's status. Whether it fires is a scheduling race, which is exactly why the identical step passed on `e24a6f0`, `1856bfb` and both PR runs. **Measured, not inferred**: piped form **3/40** runs non-zero on the real binary; no-pipe form **0/200**; deterministic repro `{ echo match; seq 1 200000; } | grep -q match` under `pipefail` → **141** every time. **THE FIRST FIX WAS WRONG AND THE STRESS ARM CAUGHT IT** — capturing to a variable is NOT sufficient, because `echo "$big" | grep -q` reproduces 141 too (the builtin writer blocks on the same pipe); the fix is **no pipe at all** (`case`). **Both** call sites converted, not just the red one: the second (`AILANG_BIN`) has never failed but **only by ACCIDENT OF SIZE** — `--version` is **167 bytes**, which fits the 64 KiB pipe buffer, so `echo` never blocks. That is a property of the output LENGTH, not of the shape; same race, one verbose release away. All three symptoms follow exactly: R3 compared the variant's legs to the control's hashes (REDs on line 444 only — the control is last, so its own reads happened to be right), R4d saw roles `['control','control']` and REDded `control reused across pairs`, and **`R4c` — the section-locality that revision round 4 was written specifically to add — was SILENTLY VACUOUS**, comparing the last block to itself. And **no deny-list-free straddle exists here**: floors AND `toolchain` directives select only UPWARD (measured — `go1.24.9`/`go1.25.6` directives all resolved to `go1.26.4`), the sole cached toolchain above the local `go1.26.4` is the deny-listed `go1.26.5`, and the control is always the local toolchain. ## STATUS 2026-08-03 (iteration 44) — **`w-ddl-gate-teeth` DG.B DESIGNED — THE VERSION PIN, AND THE FRESHNESS TEST THAT CALLED A POPULATED STORE EMPTY** (PR #34 → squash `6b8e77e`, dev CI green **both jobs SHA-addressed on the merge commit**; quorum **2 rounds, BOTH BLOCKED, narrow-refinement carve-out APPLIED as the bounded 2nd revision**; designer `codex:gpt-5.6-sol` (rotation) fired **twice** — original + the one sanctioned revision — planner/executor/evaluator **not fired**, a design iteration; **`metered=$0.3192`** of the `$5` ceiling; controller `claude-opus-5`; rotation pointer **ADVANCED to `codex:gpt-5.6-sol`**). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: A KNOWN-POSITIVE CONTROL PROVES AN INSTRUMENT *CAN* FIRE — IT DOES NOT PROVE IT FIRES *ONLY WHERE IT SHOULD*, AND THE UNTESTED HALF IS THE EXCLUSION BOUNDARY.** DG.B is the half of Mark's attended ratification that was **ratified but never designed**: `4d/OD-3` was a three-alternative decision packet with no ACs, no named mutations and no fixtures, so this iteration produced the **design**, not the code — item 4d stays open on implementation. **THE CENTRAL PROBLEM, MEASURED RATHER THAN ASSUMED**: on the day the pin lands a brand-new store and a decade-old legacy store **both** read `user_version=0` (V-C/V-M), and production performs **zero** `sqlite_master` inspection (V-E, control firing in `journal_test.go`), so nothing can tell them apart — making "reject 0" brick every existing store and "accept 0" the inert-marker shape OD-3's own text warns against. D5 resolves it by classifying **before** `schemaSQL` runs; D6 gives legacy/future/invalid **distinct typed errors**; D7 commits schema + pin in **one transaction**; D8 extends the boundary to `OpenReadOnly`; D9 freezes an **independent** version-1 ledger. **EVERY LOAD-BEARING PREMISE WAS RE-MEASURED THROUGH THE PINNED `modernc.org/sqlite v1.54.0` DRIVER RATHER THAN THE `sqlite3` C CLI** — the instrument gap iteration 43's planner recorded as **F4**, closed here by construction: rows **V-I…V-S** are controller first-party. The most load-bearing was **V-I**: `PRAGMA user_version` **IS** transactional (in-tx `1`, after `Rollback()` **`0` with 0 application objects**, control set-`5`-outside-tx reads `5`), so D7's induced-failure criterion **AC7 is achievable at all** — had the pragma escaped the transaction, the milestone would have shipped a vacuous atomicity gate. **THREE DEFECTS, AND THE QUORUM EARNED ITS COST ON EVERY ONE.** (1) **`gemini-3-1-pro` and the controller INDEPENDENTLY AND SEPARATELY** found that DG.B makes DG.A's **AC2 vacuous** — the criterion iteration 43 called *"the required discriminating M4/M5 control"*. `MUT-EXISTING-DDL-CHANGE-REMANIFESTED` alters neither `user_version` nor the application-object count, and D5 classifies on exactly those two, so after DG.B the pre-journal test returns the same typed legacy error **with the mutation applied and unapplied** — it passes both ways. gemini: *"rendering AC2 mathematically impossible to satisfy as written."* Two parties reaching one conclusion by different routes is why this is recorded as the round's finding rather than either party's. Fixed **without deletion**, per D4's own convention: AC2's original text and mutation are **retained verbatim and marked SUPERSEDED**, the vacuity stated in plain words, the mutation **re-pointed to AC10**, the "existing store never upgraded" property **reassigned to AC6** as a PRODUCTION discriminator with the argument that door-rejection is *stronger* than post-open DDL comparison because no typed `Store` escapes, and AC2 given its own new production RED `MUT-LEGACY-REJECTION-BYPASS`. (2) **`gpt5-6-sol`: D5's case table (0 / 1 / >1) was NOT TOTAL** — a negative `user_version` matched no branch. **Measured rather than forwarded, and it came back WORSE THAN FILED**: negatives round-trip and **persist across close/reopen** (`-1`, INT32_MIN), **and the field truncates SILENTLY** — `2147483648` and `4294967296` both succeed and read back **`0`** (V-N…V-R). So a store stamped by a future binary above INT32_MAX classifies as *version 0*, and if structurally empty would be **initialized and stamped version 1** — the detector erasing its own evidence. That truncation is a **controller finding, not the reviewer's**. Fixed: the table is now total and mutually exclusive over the signed-32-bit domain (invalid / fresh / legacy / supported / future), a new `InvalidSchemaVersionError`, AC12 + AC13, and a Design Freeze bullet pinning every schema version to `1 .. 2147483647` with the truncation measurement as the stated reason. (3) **`gpt5-6-sol` round 2: THE FRESHNESS PREDICATE ITSELF WAS UNSOUND, AND ITS `catch` LANDED ON MY OWN VERIFICATION ROW.** In SQL `LIKE`, `_` is a **single-character wildcard**, so `name NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%'` excludes every name beginning `sqlite` **plus any character** — not the literal reserved prefix. **V-S, measured before adopting the fix**: SQLite **rejects** `sqlite_internal_probe` (*"object name reserved for internal use"*) but **ACCEPTS** `sqliteX_probe`, and a version-0 store whose only application object is `sqliteX_probe` counts **0** under the shipped predicate — classified **FRESH**, then initialized and **version-stamped over real application data** — against **1** under `NOT LIKE 'sqlite\_%' ESCAPE '\'`, with a two-sided control confirming genuine `sqlite_` internals are still excluded and `sqliteX_probe` no longer is. **The sharp part is whose instrument failed**: my own **V-K** had *just* re-checked this predicate against the iteration-41 `sqlite_%` scar and pronounced it LIVE — because I proved the limb **fires** (a known-positive matched) and **never tested the other direction**, whether a name that should SURVIVE the filter does. A known-positive control establishes an instrument *can* match; it says nothing about what it *wrongly* matches. **→ Gate-5 process fix this iteration.** **CARVE-OUT, APPLIED DELIBERATELY AND RECORDED**: round 2 was `gemini-3-1-pro` **PASS** + controller **PASS** + one reject whose `proposed_fix` was concrete, reviewer-authored, and disputed **no part of the direction** — a one-predicate soundness bug plus fixtures — so the narrow-refinement carve-out applied (as at iters 13 and 40) and the reviewer's fix was taken **verbatim** as AC14 + `MUT-PREFIX-WILDCARD-REGRESSION`, not paraphrased. **THE HONEST TALLY WAS CORRECTED TWICE ACROSS THE CYCLE — 6/11 → 9/13 → 10/14 production discriminators** — because Objection 1 was itself a tally-honesty failure, and the doc now carries **18** premise rows and **9** primary named mutations. **CF-O-1, FOUND WHILE APPLYING THE FIX AND RECORDED RATHER THAN SILENTLY WIDENED INTO SCOPE**: the same unescaped predicate sits in **LANDED DG.A code** at `journal_test.go:877`, so AC1's *"unexpected tables fail loudly"* cannot see a `sqliteX_probe` table — impact low (hardcoded seven-table manifest, one site, control-verified), but it means iteration 41's V-row calling that filter a harmless *"dormant guard"* was **understated**. Left for DG.B's executor, who edits adjacent code. **PARKED FOR MARK — ONE NEW ASK, `4d/OD-7`**: DG.B may be implemented and mutation-tested, but **rollout onto a rig holding valuable version-0 stores is blocked** — the repository cannot establish whether the rig's existing stores are disposable, reconstructible or authoritative, and blindly stamping `user_version=1` would prove only that a pragma was writable and would recreate the inert-marker defect, so the design contains **no command or helper that does it**. Recommendation: inventory first, then reconstruct-if-provably-reconstructible, else a separately ratified certification milestone. **Queue: 4d DG.B [DESIGN LANDED, implementation ROUTABLE next iteration, rollout: OD-7 RATIFIED by Mark 2026-08-03 (attended, see STATUS) — verified-disposable sweep; unexpected store → re-park]; 4e RG.A and 4f branch-A remain routable and untouched** (Standing rule 1). **A GATE-4 INSTRUMENT FAILURE, CAUGHT BY ITS OWN CONTROL**: the skill's prescribed stale-base tell greps `ITERATION <N>` **uppercase** and returned **0 for the target AND 0 for the control** on this charter, which writes `(iteration 43)` **lowercase** — the control did exactly its job, reporting a broken instrument rather than a stale charter (the rotation invariant `^## STATUS 2026` read **3**, and the charter was byte-identical to origin). ## STATUS 2026-08-03 (iteration 43) — **`w-ddl-gate-teeth` DG.A LANDED — THE GATE NO LONGER INVITES ITS OWN BYPASS** (PR #33 → squash `ad619d8`, dev CI green **both jobs SHA-addressed on the merge commit and the step logs read to prove they ran** — 10 distinct Go packages `ok`, zero FAIL, 4/4 required identities across 11 modules, 14/14 named tests `failed_tests=0`; evaluator `sonnet` **PASS 91/100, ZERO blocking**; planner `opus`, executor `codex:gpt-5.6-sol` (`codex login status` → "Logged in using ChatGPT", 87.1k tokens on the subscription bucket), **designer NOT fired — rotation pointer unchanged at `claude:claude-fable-5`**; **`metered=$0.00`** — the first code-landing iteration of this mission to cost nothing metered, because the doc already carried its two quorum rounds and every role rode a quota bucket; controller `claude-opus-5`). **THE FIRST SPRINT IN FOUR ITERATIONS: 40, 41 AND 42 EACH LANDED A DOCUMENT AND PARKED, AND THE ATTENDED TRIPLE RATIFICATION UNBLOCKED ALL THREE AT ONCE.** **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: A CHANGE DETECTOR THAT COSTS ONE LINE TO SILENCE IS NOT A GATE, AND THE REPAIR IS AN ASYMMETRY, NOT A STRONGER PIN.** The old `TestPreJournalMigrationPreservesExistingDDL` derived **both sides** of its comparison from the same already-edited `schema.sql`, behind a sha256 source pin whose failure message handed the developer the new hash; pasting it back — the single action the message invites — re-greened all 10 packages with the edit unapplied to every existing store (iter-41's **M4**), and `delete(after, "journal")` meant no test owned the journal DDL at all. DG.A replaces it with a **hardcoded seven-table canonical manifest** plus `preJournalSchemaV0`, a **verbatim fixture from `8133573`** (sha256 `35f09862e2…`, structurally independent of the embedded `schemaSQL` — `//go:embed` vs a compile-time `const`, so no runtime path can derive one from the other). **THE LOAD-BEARING MEASUREMENT, REPRODUCED FIRST-PARTY BY THE CONTROLLER AND THEN AGAIN BY THE EVALUATOR RATHER THAN INHERITED FROM THE EXECUTOR**: apply a real `store_heads` DDL edit **and legitimately re-manifest it** — the same one-line update the old pin invited — and the fresh-store gate goes **GREEN** while `TestOpenAddsJournalAndDetectsStalePreJournalDDL` **REDS** at `journal_test.go:870` naming `store_heads`, printing got (2 cols) vs want (3 cols). That asymmetry *is* the de-fang Mark ratified, and it is why the fix is not "a better hash": no single edit re-greens both halves, because the historical fixture is not derived from the thing being edited. **RATIFICATION SCOPE — THE HALF THAT IS NOT DESIGNED, AND WHY IT WAS NOT BUILT.** Mark's stamp ratified **two** things: the sha256 de-fang **and** a `PRAGMA user_version` pin failing LOUD on binary↔store mismatch, with the frozen-store touch ratified. The doc's D4 position was **retained verbatim as the argument that lost** rather than quietly rewritten to look prescient, and `gpt5-6-sol` — who rejected twice on this exact direction — is recorded as having been right. **A LIVE OD-NUMBER COLLISION THAT INVERTS A RATIFICATION, FOUND BY READING THE STAMP CAREFULLY RATHER THAN QUICKLY.** Mark's single sentence contains *"4d RATIFIED — fix NOW: … `user_version` pin"* **and** *"OD-3 stays declined-as-primary"*. Both are true and they are about **different items**: `OD-3` means the `user_version` contract in 4d's doc and *"change the two `scan.go` sites"* in 4e's, and `OD-4` likewise means two different things. ## STATUS 2026-08-03 (iteration 42) — **`w-bench-load-confound` (item 4f): DESIGN DOC LANDED; THE ITEM ITSELF PARKED `needs-human-review` ON OD-6 — THE PAIR RECORDS THE LOAD AND THE GATE THAT BLESSES IT NEVER READS IT** (PR #31 → squash `b986c7a`, dev CI green **both jobs SHA-addressed on the merge commit and the step logs read to prove they ran** — Z3 4.16.0 present, 4/4 required identities across 11 modules, 14/14 named tests `failed_tests=0`, bench-smoke name manifest PASSED, **10 distinct Go packages `ok`, zero FAIL**; quorum **2 rounds, r1 BLOCKED both-reject, r2 BLOCKED 1-pass/1-reject**, narrow-refinement carve-out **deliberately NOT applied**; `metered=$0.2104`; designer `claude:claude-fable-5` fired twice — original + the one sanctioned revision — planner/executor/evaluator **not fired**; controller `claude-opus-5`). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: THE RECORDER CAPTURES THE LOAD AND THE GATE THAT BLESSES THE PAIR NEVER READS IT.** Item 4f's design is good — machine-emitted conditions, all-or-nothing refusal, the A/B mandate expressed as **file grammar** rather than prose. But R4, the rule that decides a pair is a valid cost claim, constrains only `control.commit == variant.parent` plus identical `goversion`, `goos_goarch`, `ncpu`, `hw_model`. **A search of the whole document for any temporal, load-comparability, pair-identity or control-reuse constraint returns NOTHING** (only the words "reuse"/"collision" in the Conflict Surface's own column headers), while the **known-positive control in the same call** confirms the schema *does* carry `utc`, `load_before`, `load_after`. So an idle variant pairs with a control recorded days earlier under heavy load and **passes** — reintroducing the **6.06×** artefact class inside the artifact built to eliminate it. **SEVENTH INSTANCE of this mission's signature shape, and its THIRD inside this one document's evolution**: round 1 carried it as the caller-cwd toolchain probe (a gate comparing a value to itself), the revision fixed that and **kept the bug as a named mutation** (`MUT-PROBE-CALLER-DIR`, which must *green* a known-cross-toolchain pair), and round 2 surfaced the same shape one level up in the pairing rule itself. **BOTH ROUND-1 OBJECTIONS WERE RIGHT AND BOTH ADOPTED.** `gpt5-6-sol`: the recorder ran `go test -bench` with no wall-clock ceiling (Standing Rule 6), and `go test -timeout` is **insufficient** — it bounds the test binary's clock, not the compile step or a wedged child; fixed by **mirroring `run_bounded` from `scripts/verify_ail.sh:61-74`** (read first-party: python3 `start_new_session`, `os.killpg` SIGKILL, exit 124, hardcoded deadlines), timeout = named refusal emitting **zero fences**, `MUT-REC-STALL` proving the kill reaches a **grandchild**. `gemini-3-1-pro`: `go env GOVERSION` was probed in the caller's directory — **and I MEASURED THE PREMISE RATHER THAN FORWARDING IT**, which made it sharper than filed: with `GOTOOLCHAIN=auto` live, a module with floor `go 1.26.5` reads **`go1.26.5`**, this repo (floor 1.26.4) reads **`go1.26.4`**, no module reads `go1.26.4` — so `GOVERSION` is **directory-sensitive**, and because **OD-1 proposes lowering this repo's floor**, the first A/B pair straddling it is *exactly* the cross-toolchain pair R4 exists to red, which the round-1 design would have greened by comparing the variant's toolchain to itself. Fix verified with a control in the same call (`go -C <module floor 1.26.5> env GOVERSION` → `go1.26.5`). **THE DESIGNER SELF-CAUGHT THE ITERATION-40 TRAP**: its first draft of the bounding fix bounded only the `go` invocations and left `$AILANG_BIN --version` **unbounded inside the remedy for unbounded waits** — found in its own re-read, now bounded in D1, the deadline bullet and the Design Freeze. Doc 514 → 673 → 793 lines; Verification Log 22 → 25 rows; named mutations 7 → 10. **WHY IT PARKS.** The carve-out requires every remaining objection to be non-directional with a single reviewer-authored fix; `gpt5-6-sol` says the document's **central claim** is contradicted and demands *"verify or retract the unsupported premise"*, and its `proposed_fix` **branches two ways that deliver different items** — **A** single-session interleaved `--record-pair` with a pair ID and control-reuse rejection (keeps the promise, ~0.6–0.9 d, outgrows the row's sizing) vs **B** keep the mechanism and weaken the claim to *"mechanically complete evidence, not a mechanically valid cost claim"*. Choosing is a scope judgment → **OD-6**, recommended **branch A bounded**, taking **neither** the reviewer's third limb (a *measured load-divergence acceptance rule*) — no data on this rig can defensibly derive that threshold and it is the noise-gate `BASELINE.md:7-8` already calls **dishonest (S6)**, wearing a new name: **a reviewer being right about the defect does not make it right about the prescription** (iter-41's `sqlite_%` lesson). **THREE OF MY OWN INSTRUMENTS FAILED AND EVERY ONE WAS CAUGHT BY A CONTROL OR AN IMPOSSIBLE READING**: (i) a **mislabelled control** — a persisted `cd` made the line labelled *"CONTROL: the repo"* run inside the temp module, returning `go1.26.5` for a repo whose floor is 1.26.4, caught only because the reading was **impossible** (Gate-2 rule 4(a), in my own hands one gate after quoting it); (ii) `grep -c "ok(\\t| +)github"` over the CI log returned **0** — the iter-41 scar one variant deeper, since `go test` prints `ok` + **two spaces** + a **TAB**; true reading **10 packages**; (iii) a grep for the quorum blocks returned **0** because the heading wraps the path in **backticks**; both blocks were there. **Queue: 4f [PARKED needs-human-review on OD-6]** — doc + 25 verification rows + 10 named mutations landed, no milestone routable; **CF-K-2 is now FOLDED INTO the doc** (R4's toolchain limb + `MUT-CLAIM-TOOLCHAIN-SPLIT`/`MUT-AB-FLOOR-SPLIT`) rather than free-floating. ## STATUS 2026-07-30 (iteration 41) — **`w-ddl-gate-teeth` (item 4d): DOC + FIVE MEASUREMENTS LANDED; THE REPAIR ITSELF IS PARKED ON A COLLISION BETWEEN TWO RATIFIED GUARDRAILS** (PR #30 → squash `d56da6f`, dev CI green **both jobs SHA-addressed on the merge commit and the step logs read to prove they ran**; quorum **2 rounds, both BLOCKED, carve-out deliberately NOT applied**; `metered=$0.1155`; designer `codex:gpt-5.6-sol` fired twice — original + the one sanctioned revision — planner/executor/evaluator **not fired**; controller `claude-opus-5`). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: A GATE WHOSE SANCTIONED REPAIR RE-GREENS IT IS NOT A GATE — THE DOCUMENTED FIX IS THE VULNERABILITY.** Five iterations of this mission have hunted *gates that cannot fail*; item 4d's gate is a **different and worse species**. `TestPreJournalMigrationPreservesExistingDDL` pins the sha256 of `schema.sql`'s pre-journal prefix, so a real DDL edit **does** red — with a message handing the developer the new hash. Paste it back, the single action the message invites, and **all 10 packages go green while the edit remains unapplied to every existing store**. That is **M4 `MUT-DDL-DRIFT-REPINNED`**, new this iteration; the queue row carried M1–M3 and stopped one step short of the thing that makes them matter. **A change detector that costs one line to silence is not a correctness gate, and its green is worse than no gate because the green gets cited.** **FIVE MEASUREMENTS, FIRST-PARTY AT `e5027df`, EACH REVERTED BY `cp` WITH sha256 PRINTED BOTH SIDES; THE THREE INHERITED CLAIMS ALL HOLD** but were re-measured rather than restated, the row's evidence being from `0f2afad` with three milestones landed since. **M1** widening journal's `kind` CHECK leaves `go test ./...` **rc=0, 10/10 green** — and the green was **proven to be blindness, not a no-op mutation**, by a `sqlite3` probe reading the materialized DDL **containing `MUTANT`** with the unmutated backup as the control in the same call (`store.go:262` execs `schemaSQL` verbatim, so it is what production ships). **M2** a real `store_heads` column reds **exactly one test**, at `journal_test.go:636` — the source pin, which `t.Fatalf`s **before line 638 opens a database**, so the `sqlite_master` comparison the doc-set credits **never executes**. **M3** short-circuiting that comparison (both vars used, `go vet` rc=0) leaves package and full suite **green** — zero discrimination. **M5**, the one that shows the stakes: an old store opened by a binary built from the new schema, through **real `store.Open`**, returns **`err=nil`** with the DDL **byte-identical before and after** — *not applied* is indistinguishable from *applied*. The comparison is **self-referential by construction** (`before` comes from the same already-edited source `Open` will exec) so it can only ever fire for a newly added non-journal table, and `journal` is excluded by an explicit `delete(after, "journal")`. **WHY IT PARKS, AND WHY THAT IS THE CHARTER WORKING RATHER THAN A STALL.** `gpt5-6-sol` rejected in both rounds on a **direction** dispute — sharpened the second time to *"axiom noncompliance, not merely an out-of-scope enhancement"* — so the narrow-refinement carve-out, which requires every remaining objection to be non-directional, **does not apply and was not stretched**. What is actually in front of the loop is a collision between **two ratified guardrails**: the **no-silent-fallback axiom** (shipping a test-only detector closes the item while production still returns success for a structurally stale store) and **frozen-kernel discipline** (changing which on-disk stores `store.Open` accepts requires explicit human ratification). Standing rule 2 forbids forcing past the objection; the charter forbids adopting the reviewer's fix headlessly. **A headless loop must not pick a winner between two rules Mark ratified** — so it became **OD-5**, with both sides at full strength and a recommendation, and the reviewer is **not wrong on the facts** (I measured the fail-open; until OD-3 ships, deployed binaries cannot diagnose drift) — only about who may decide. **THE QUORUM'S LEDGER IS THE ARGUMENT FOR RUNNING IT: FOUR OBJECTIONS, THREE RIGHT, ONE WRONG IN ITS PRESCRIPTION.** Right: fixture provenance, durable-table enumeration, determinism (Go map iteration is randomised), and — **r2, load-bearing, and neither the designer nor I had checked it** — whether the historical artifact's DDL still matches HEAD, because if it did not the proposed test would **RED ON BASELINE** and the design would be broken as written (measured: **all 6 tables identical, zero drift**, premise HOLDS). Wrong: `gemini-3-1-pro` asked to log **`name NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%'`** as `tableDDL`'s filter, but on a materialized store `sqlite_master` holds **14** rows — 7 tables plus **7 `sqlite_autoindex_*` of `type='index'`** — so `count(type='table' AND name NOT LIKE 'sqlite_%')` and `count(type='table')` are **both 7**: that clause excludes **nothing today** and the live limb is `type='table'`. Adopting it on the reviewer's authority would have **logged a dead mechanism as the live one — iteration 38's exact defect, reproduced inside the remedy for it.** Separately, `gpt5-6-sol`'s provenance ask **improved on what both the doc and the reviewer proposed**: `d5774eb` added the journal table, so `8133573:host/store/schema.sql` is the last pre-journal schema (sha256 `35f09862e2…`, 6 tables) and the fixture is now sourced **verbatim from a pinned real artifact** instead of hand-authored. **AN HONESTY GAP NEITHER REVIEWER NAMED**: the designer's D2 test was `TestOpenUpgradesPreJournalStoreToCurrentDDL` and would **pass** while `store.Open` performs no upgrade at all beyond creating the absent `journal` table — a green test whose *name* asserts the protection the design explicitly disclaims, in the artifact a future reader greps first (iter-38's class). Renamed **`TestOpenAddsJournalAndDetectsStalePreJournalDDL`**, honest claim required **beside the test**, not only in the doc's tail. **A NEW ZSH INSTRUMENT DEFECT — THIRD IN THIS CLASS, AND IT RETURNED A PLAUSIBLE NUMBER RATHER THAN AN ERROR.** `git show "$c:host/store/schema.sql"` reported **`total_tables=0` for the commit that created the schema**: zsh parses `$c:h` as the **`:h` dirname modifier**, so the path became `.ost/store/schema.sql`. Isolated with a **bash control** on identical strings (zsh 5.9: `:h`→`.ost/x`, `:t`, `:r`, `:e` all bite; `"$c:store/x"` is a hard `bad substitution`; bash leaves all five literal). Fix: `"${c}:host/…"`. It matters **here** because `git show "$rev:path"` is the canonical archaeology idiom, the shared skill's Gate 1 prescribes that very form, **every Go file in this repo lives under `host/`**, and with stderr redirected the failure becomes a plausible `grep -c` zero. Scoped honestly: **no committed script uses `git show` at all** (control-verified), so it is a loop-instrument defect, not a defect in landed code. **A MEASUREMENT COST OF THE PARKED OD-1 THAT NOBODY HAD PRICED**: every local `host/store` measurement now requires **editing `go.mod`**, because `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.6` is refused outright (`go.mod requires go >= 1.26.4`) — the `go` directive is a **floor**, so the corrective toolchain is **unselectable** while OD-1 sits parked. Every number in this iteration is conditioned on a throwaway worktree with the floor lowered, stated rather than glossed; it is a recurring per-iteration tax and now a cost row on OD-1 (**CF-L-3**). **Four of my own instruments failed and every one was caught by a control or assertion I ran on purpose** — the `:h` defect (first symptom hidden by my own `2>/dev/null`), a `grep -cE "ok  +github"` returning **0** against a 69 KB CI log because `go test` prints `ok`+**TAB** (true reading: **10 packages ok**), an empty `--unread` control that was a wrong pattern rather than a broken tool, and a Gate-4 doc edit that **refused to apply** because its anchor's trailing double-space had been stripped — the `assert count==1` guard caught it instead of silently mis-editing. **Queue: 4d [PARKED needs-human-review on OD-5] — doc + evidence landed, DG.A specified but NOT routable; 4f `w-bench-load-confound` is now the only unblocked head item**, deliberately left again because its CF-K-2 would bank a toolchain condition that OD-1 is parked to invalidate. New carry-forwards **CF-L-1** (DG.A, blocked on OD-5), **CF-L-2** (`schema_ddl_test.go` split, cosmetic), **CF-L-3** (the `go.mod`-floor tax → OD-1). Rotation pointer **ADVANCED to `codex:gpt-5.6-sol`** (a designer that ran advances it). 4 stamps → newest 3 kept (iter-41, iter-40, iter-39); the positionally-4th (iter-38) archived this Gate 4.

## STATUS 2026-07-30 (iteration 40) — **`w-race-gate-blindspot` (item 4e): MECHANISM IDENTIFIED — IT IS A GO COMPILER REGRESSION, NOT A SQLITE BUG, AND IT DOES NOT NEED `-race`** (PR #29 → squash `c90713b`, dev CI green SHA-addressed on the merge commit; doc + re-runnable reproduction fixture LANDED; **remediation PARKED `needs-human-review` on OD-1/OD-2**; quorum **2 rounds, both BLOCKED, carve-out revision applied**, `metered=$0.123`; planner/executor/designer **not fired** — an investigation iteration; controller `claude-opus-5`). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: THE COMPILER IS AN INSTRUMENT TOO, AND NOTHING IN THIS REPOSITORY WAS CHECKING IT.** Item 4e had sat for three iterations as *"a test that fails deterministically because the gate that would see it is never run"*, with the mechanism labelled UNKNOWN and a standing hypothesis of `modernc.org/sqlite`'s `unsafe` usage interacting with `-race`'s memory layout. **That hypothesis is REFUTED.** go **1.26.0 → 1.26.5** — 1.26.5 being the **latest stable**, so there is nothing to upgrade forward to — miscompile, on darwin/arm64, a **local array literal indexed by a `range` variable** assigned into a struct string field inside a composite literal that also carries an interface method call: the field comes back with a **corrupt string header** (nil data pointer, garbage length `4334851712`), so merely printing it SIGSEGVs. **A 52-line program with ZERO dependencies and NO `-race` reproduces it under a plain `go build`**; `-gcflags=all=-N` fixes it, `all=-l` does not (the optimizer, not inlining); go1.25.6 and go1.24.9 are clean. **ONE mechanism explains BOTH of iter-39's two symptoms** — the same one-package `-N` switch clears the *failure* of `TestScanUnreadableLogKeysetResumes` **and** the *hang* of `TestScanUnreadableWorldsFindsPoison` — which is exactly what iter-39 demanded of any candidate. Census: **exactly two** production sites carry the shape, `host/store/scan.go:74` and `:112`, which are precisely the two functions the two symptoms belong to; **`scan.go` is correct Go and needs no correctness fix.** And the `-race` leg the item was asking for is **viable and cheap**: with `GOTOOLCHAIN=go1.25.6` + `AILANG_BIN` set, `go test ./... -race` is **rc=0, 10/10 packages, 179 s, ZERO data races** (`host/store` **17.1 s green** against **FAIL + hang at 124.7 s** on go1.26.4, same worktree, minutes apart) — so *"just add `-race`"* was correct all along and for the wrong reason: the blocker was never the race detector. **FOUR OF MY OWN INSTRUMENTS OR CLAIMS FAILED, AND THREE WERE CAUGHT BY CONTROLS I RAN ON PURPOSE.** (i) I ran `GOARCH=amd64 go build && ./amd64 | sed …`, got **empty output with rc=0**, and was about to record *"amd64 is unaffected"* — the binary had never run (`bad CPU type in executable`; no Rosetta on this rig). That is Gate-2 rule 3a's vacuous-pass class, live, in my own hands, one iteration after the sibling loop paid for the same thing; **amd64 scope stays UNDETERMINED** and AC6 settles it in CI. (ii) My first full-repo `-race` sweep reported two FAILs I was about to attribute to `-race`; a no-`-race` control showed the broker one fails **either way** with `AILANG_BIN must name the pinned released interpreter` — I had simply not exported it, and that is the **M6/B1 anti-false-green guard working exactly as designed**. Both FAILs vanished once it was set. (iii) A verbatim test-only copy of the miscompiled loop **passed** under `-race`, because my diagnostic `t.Logf` calls perturbed the optimizer away — the heisenbug signature that pointed at the compiler in the first place. Both r2 objections carried concrete reviewer-authored fixes and disputed no part of the **direction**, so the **narrow-refinement carve-out** applied for the bounded 2nd revision. **The lesson: a revision is not a smaller change than an original, and deserves the same adversarial read.** **PARKED FOR MARK, and nothing about the build changed this iteration:** **OD-1** (lower `go.mod` 1.26.4 → 1.25.6 — ratification-class; recommended **YES**: the newest stable Go miscompiles a shape in landed durability code, there is no fixed release to move to, and the only dependency constraint `modernc.org/sqlite` needs just 1.25.0) and **OD-2** (file the 52-line reproducer upstream at **`golang/go`** — a public post to a third-party project, which is outside anything this loop is authorised to do; recommended **YES**, repro ready to paste). Also measured: `go.mod` **cannot** pin an older toolchain at all — both directives are floors, only `GOTOOLCHAIN` selects exactly — so the pin must live in CI's env and the developer's, and `verify_go.sh`'s job is to **verify, not to set**. (ii) **OQ-5's premise was FALSE, and OQ-5 is a STOP instruction**: it justifies its default by *"the caller-supplied logical clock the broker **already threads for records**"*, but `LogicalTime` appears **zero times** in `host/broker` production code and `EffectRecord` has no such field — unexamined, the executor would have been CORRECT to stop and the milestone would have died at checkpoint one. The conclusion survives its false premise: `EffectRequest.Now` is caller-supplied, in scope in `Invoke`, already feeds `requestHash` and capability expiry, and `time.Now` appears nowhere in `host/broker` production code while `EffectRequest` is constructed **only** in `_test.go` files — so `req.Now` is a logical value by construction. Resolved and handed down **with its evidence**. (iii) **The plan's own verify command prescribes a known-red gate** (`-race` over `host/store`). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE, AS FIRST WRITTEN — THEN RETRACTED IN THE SAME GATE 5 (see the correction at the end of this stamp): `git checkout <file>` IS NOT A MUTATION REVERT — IT IS A MILESTONE DELETION WEARING ONE.** Reproducing the headline gate myself (`MUT-INTENT-AFTER-DISPATCH`, **2 red / 99 green**, message `state "not-started" … want indeterminate`, matching the executor exactly), the revert step `git checkout host/broker/broker.go` restored from **HEAD** — and every sprint in this mission is delivered as an **UNCOMMITTED** worktree diff, because the codex sandbox cannot commit. So it silently discarded the milestone's entire `broker.go` and left a **green suite running on `origin/dev`'s code**. Caught **only** by the sha256 baseline the mutation discipline already demanded for a different reason (`99f3568…` → `6bc8514…`); recovered from the executor's own logged `git diff` and proven restored by **two independent hashes** — `git hash-object` returning the diff's recorded post-image `1fb60d78…` and sha256 returning `99f3568…`. **The rule: when the baseline is uncommitted work, the only safe revert is a copy taken before the mutation; the sha256 is what tells you which of the two you actually did.** This extends the iter-35 mutation-as-instrument discipline to its **other end** — validity covers applying a mutation AND undoing it. **A SECOND INSTRUMENT OF MY OWN CAME BACK CLEAN BECAUSE IT COULD NOT HAVE COME BACK DIRTY**: I reported "ZERO skips" from `go test ./...` piped through `grep -c -- "--- SKIP"`, but `--- SKIP` lines print **only under `-v`** — the zero was structural, not observed. Same family as iter-37's glob-mangled `--include=*.go` and the zsh-empty `${PIPESTATUS[0]}`. **ONE MUTATION NAME, TWO FORMS, TWO ANSWERS — AND THIS TIME THE JUDGE HAD THE CONFOUNDED ONE.** Executor: `MUT-OUTCOME-BEFORE-RECORD` **2 red / 99 green**; judge, independently: **18 red**, every success path failing `refuses to persist an invalid ref ""`, concluding the form needs fault injection. Reading both applied patches settles it — the executor recomputed `recordObject(rec).Hash` so the outcome carried a **valid** ref and the mutation isolated the **ordering**; the judge passed the still-zero `recordRef`, so the store's own ref validator fired first and swamped the signal. **The executor's form is faithful, the plan's prediction stands, and CF-MJB-6 is REFUTED.** Fourth "one NAME two FORMS" instance (after `MUT-BENCH-DROP` iter-34, `MUT-PENDING-UNBOUNDED` iter-35) and the first where the **judge** ran the coarser form — a mutation's prose "form:" line does not determine the instrument; only the applied diff does. **THE JUDGE'S FINDING WAS REPRODUCED BEFORE BEING ACCEPTED, AND IT WAS UNDER-STATED.** CF-MJB-1 (filed non-blocking) says `recover.go`'s comment omits the Decision-5 residual. Every gate re-run **OUTSIDE** the codex sandbox (the executor correctly labelled its own `verify_go.sh` UNINFORMATIVE UNDER SANDBOX with the verbatim bind error): `go build`/`go vet ./...`/`gofmt` clean, `go test ./... -count=1 -v` **rc=0, 180 PASS / 0 FAIL / 2 pre-existing SKIP across 10 packages**, `verify_go.sh` rc=0, `verify_ail.sh` rc=0 (4/4, 11 modules, 14 tests), broker-only `-race` rc=0, both forbidden-path assertions rc=0 **with a known-positive control**. **Queue: 4c [IN-SPRINT] — MJ.A + MJ.B LANDED, MJ.C is the last milestone**; **4d `w-ddl-gate-teeth`** and **4e `w-race-gate-blindspot`** still queued, and 4e's row is re-measured this iteration (the sibling package `host/broker` under `-race` is **GREEN in 4.095 s**, so the defect is `host/store`-local). New **CF-MJB-1…CF-MJB-6** (CF-MJB-1 DONE, CF-MJB-6 REFUTED); **CF-MJA-4/CF-MJA-5 were MJ.B-owned and are NOT discharged — they roll to MJ.C, stated rather than dropped**. Designer **not fired**; rotation pointer unchanged at `claude:claude-fable-5`. **THE RETRACTION, AT THE SAME VOLUME (Gate 5, after the above had already shipped to the log, this stamp and the public report): THE `git checkout` FINDING IS NOT NEW — IT IS A RECURRENCE, AND THE RECURRENCE IS THE BETTER FINDING.** This charter already forbade it **twice** — the mutation protocol's step 5 (*"revert by `cp` from a backup taken first — never `git checkout --` on a file carrying uncommitted work (iter-34 destroyed an executor's work that way)"*) and the M3.C paragraph concluding *"the instrument must not be able to delete the thing it measures"*. Same idiom, same class of file, **four iterations later**. So: **a written rule is not a control.** What caught it at iter-34 and again here was the **sha256**, not the prose — the hash pair is the control, the prose is a reminder, and a mutation record without both printed values is unverified. A rule that must be recalled at the moment of use will eventually not be, so the durable form belongs in the **command** (take the backup in the same call that applies the mutation) rather than in a document. And iter-34 had also already recorded the check that would have caught this over-claim — *"before calling anything an Nth instance of a known class, grep the repo's own rows"* — which I ran one gate too late. The charter bullet is reframed from a new rule to a recurrence; **the Gate-4 record and the GitHub report both shipped "new finding" first and are corrected in place.** 4 stamps → newest 3 kept (iter-38, iter-37, iter-36); the positionally-4th (iter-35) archived this Gate 4.

## STATUS 2026-07-29 (iteration 36) — **`w-effect-journal` (item 4c) NEW-DOC LANDED + QUORUM-CLEARED** (PR #25 → squash `fe582b5`, dev CI green **both jobs, SHA-addressed on the merge commit**; designer `claude:claude-fable-5` on the rotation lane, quota bucket; quorum metered **$0.164** against the $5 ceiling; **no sprint routed — this was a design iteration**). **THE ITERATION'S SPINE: THE QUEUE ROW'S OWN COSTING CLAIM WAS FALSE, AND THE GATE IT CITED AS PROOF IS INERT.** The M3 planner costed this item as *"Cheaper than assumed — **no schema change** (the commit shape lives in the payload codec, not the DDL, so AC13's `sqlite_master` gate stays green)"* — a claim its own `plan.json` labels **"Planner's measured note"**, restated in the charter queue row and carried into the ratification record. The premise is TRUE (the eight commit refs do live in the payload codec, `journal.go:104-143`); the **conclusion does not follow**, because the journal table's *kind vocabulary* and its *cardinality* live in the DDL. Measured first-party at `0f2afad`: **P1** `INSERT … kind='effect-intent'` → `CHECK constraint failed: kind IN ('intent','outcome')`; **P2** a second `kind='intent'` for the same `invocation_id` → `UNIQUE constraint failed: journal.invocation_id, journal.kind`; **P3, the sharp one** — a widened CHECK re-applied to an **existing** store returns **rc=0, no error**, `sqlite_master` shows the DDL **unchanged**, and the new-kind insert then fails on the OLD constraint. With **zero** migration machinery anywhere in `host/` (no `user_version`, no `ALTER TABLE`), **any DDL change in this repo ships FAIL-OPEN** — new stores get the new schema, every existing store silently keeps the old one, nothing detects the disagreement. *A schema change that was never applied is indistinguishable from one that was* — **iteration 35's spine, one layer down, at the schema instead of the mutation.** **AND THE CITED GATE HAS NO TEETH THERE — three compiling mutations, reverted byte-identical**: `MUT-JOURNAL-DDL-WIDEN` leaves `go test ./...` **rc=0 across all 10 packages** (*nothing* in the repo guards the journal DDL); the gate's OWN `MUT-DDL-DRIFT` does red but — **read the MESSAGE, not the exit code** (the iter-34 rule paying off) — via the **sha256 source pin**, which `t.Fatalf`s before a database is built, so the `sqlite_master` comparison never runs; and `MUT-DDL-COMPARE-DEAD` (short-circuiting that comparison, both vars still used, `go vet` rc=0) leaves the whole package **GREEN**. The gate's real teeth are a source-text pin over the pre-journal *prefix* — genuine protection for pre-existing tables, delivered by a different mechanism than the one it is documented as having, and covering neither the journal table nor the upgrade path. **FIFTH instance of this mission's signature shape** (a gate no production change could fail) and the **first found at PICK time**, in a gate inherited from an item already COMPLETE and judged **zero-blocking** — exactly the exposure the iter-32 process fix names. Raised as its own queue row **4d `w-ddl-gate-teeth`**, not smuggled into 4c. **THE DESIGN**: Path A, DDL-free — per-effect synthetic IDs in a reserved `effect:<episodeID>:<ordinal>` namespace, two payload codecs, reusing the existing `kind` vocabulary; Path B (new kind labels) REJECTED on the fail-open evidence, since it would honestly cost a migration mechanism first (~2.5–3d, not ~1.5d). **TWO QUORUM ROUNDS, TWO COLLISIONS IN THE SAME FIELD.** r1 `gemini-3-1-pro` **reject**: the in-memory ordinal counter is never re-initialized after a crash, so a resumed broker's first dispatch collides with the durable `effect:<episodeID>:0` and **bricks the episode** — r0 had *frozen* the opposite claim ("collision-free by construction"), which is FALSE across a crash boundary and was corrected everywhere it was restated. `gpt5-6-sol` was **ABSENT — `budget`**, a pre-flight refusal at *"$0.1005 exceeds cap $0.1000"*, zero spend, degraded N−1 and named (never a silent pass). The r1 fix **declined the reviewer's own proposed variant with a reason**: `len(episode.History)` counts *records*, and an indeterminate effect is precisely an intent whose record was lost, so it still collides after exactly the AC7 crash. r2 (cap raised to $0.25 so the absentee could run; **both present**) `gemini-3-1-pro` **PASS**, `gpt5-6-sol` **reject** — the r1 fix is **not atomic**: derive-then-append are separate operations, so *"the resumption fix merely replaces the restart collision with a TOCTOU collision."* Applied **VERBATIM** under the charter's **narrow-refinement carve-out** (concrete reviewer-authored fix; determinism-only; direction endorsed — Path A, the journal shape, intent-before-dispatch and the ID namespace all accepted): one transactional `AppendNextEffectIntent(episodeID, intentWithoutID) (id, ordinal, err)` mints the ordinal **inside** the appending transaction, `AppendEffectIntent` and `NextEffectOrdinal` are both REMOVED, the broker holds **no ordinal state at all**, `MaxInt64` exhaustion gets a structured error, and the reviewer's two named tests land as **AC7b** with its evidence ask as **AC7c**; `gemini`'s `GetReceipt` namespace guard adopted too, **closing OD1**. Nothing was force-passed and no objection was overridden — each was *satisfied*. **V28 — HALF THE OBJECTION IS REFUTED BY LANDED CODE, AND THE FIX WAS APPLIED IN FULL ANYWAY**: two broker *processes* cannot share a store as writers (`store.Open`'s non-waiting exclusive lock → `*WriterAlreadyActive`, proven cross-process), but the in-process half is REAL — **zero mutexes** in `store.go`/`journal.go`, so two goroutines can interleave freely. *Narrowing a fix to the part of an objection you can refute is how a real defect survives a review.* **V29 — A PROCESS FINDING AGAINST MYSELF**: my own premise-row additions (V21–V25) pushed the doc to ~13,952 input tokens and tripped round 1's per-reviewer cap, costing that round the very reviewer that later found the TOCTOU — **enriching a doc degraded its own review**. Also: **my Gate-3b poll was a broken instrument of my own making** — `target=$(git rev-parse A || cd B && git rev-parse HEAD)` binds as `(A || cd) && HEAD`, so `$target` held TWO SHAs, every API call was malformed, and the loop printed blanks toward its deadline; the iter-24/iter-107 meta-rule ("use the shipped snippet verbatim; a hand-rolled variant is a new defect surface") caught in the act, third instance. Re-run with a single literal SHA: **both jobs `completed/success`** on PR head `de0fb59` and again on merge commit `fe582b5`. Designer-verified numbers **re-measured by the controller rather than cited** (V25/V25b): `storejournal.ail` **7/7 contracts verified**, **`len(tests[]) == 30`**, `passed_tests == 37` reported separately; and Path A's mechanism confirmed on **`modernc.org/sqlite`**, not just the designer's sqlite3 CLI. **Queue: 4c [NEXT — QUORUM-CLEARED, routes straight to sprint-planner]; new item 4d `w-ddl-gate-teeth` queued.** Designer rotation advanced `codex:gpt-5.6-sol` → `claude:claude-fable-5`. 4 stamps → newest 3 kept (iter-36, iter-35, iter-34); the positionally-4th (iter-32) archived this Gate 4.

## STATUS 2026-07-28 (iteration 24) — **`w-mcp-projection` DESIGN DOC WRITTEN + TWO QUORUM ROUNDS + carve-out revision → LANDED as a record, and the item RE-TAGGED `BLOCKED` on three named prerequisites. The clause-6 boundary cannot be built on v0.30.0, and we now know exactly why.** Item 4 `w-effect-broker-m3` stayed PARKED (no `@MarkEdmondson1234` answer yet), so the pick was the queue's next actionable item — **item 5 `w-mcp-projection`**, the NEW-DOC lane. Grep confirmed the NEW-DOC tag was a FACT (no doc existed; `design_docs/planned/` held only `w-effect-broker-m3` + `w-log-epoch-decision`). Designer = **`codex:gpt-5.6-sol`** (rotation: last-used was `claude:claude-fable-5`, so codex was next) → `design_docs/planned/w-mcp-projection.md`, **641 lines**. **NO SPRINT RAN, DELIBERATELY — and that is the finding, not a failure.** **THE ITEM'S OWN PREMISE DID NOT SURVIVE CONTACT WITH THE BINARY.** The queue row read "reuse `ailang serve-api --mcp/--a2a` machinery — do not reinvent · ~1d". The charter's Conflict Surface had already, correctly, split that into premises (protocol projection of static `.ail` exports, live-tested 2026-07-23) versus **acceptance criteria** (dynamic worldd-backed registry, per-session capability filtering, propose→verify→commit enforcement) — and this iteration proved the acceptance half is **not reachable on v0.30.0 at all**. **THE LOAD-BEARING DISCOVERY: the projected surface cannot be an exact allowlist.** My own first-party stdio MCP probe against the pinned binary (not the designer's account — I re-ran it): `unfiltered → ['addOne','submit_feedback']`, `--routes-only → ['submit_feedback']`, `--caps '' → ['addOne','submit_feedback']`. So (i) a **built-in `submit_feedback` tool is exposed under EVERY flag combination and no flag removes it** — and its own tool description, which I captured verbatim, routes to a `public-feedback` inbox with a **Pub/Sub notification**, i.e. a built-in *egress* tool no World session authorized, sitting inside a bar whose clause 2 demands zero cloud dependencies in the core and whose clause 3 demands that **no ambient-authority path exist from an agent to the outside world**; (ii) discovery is built from loaded module exports rather than a caller-supplied provider, and `--caps` / `--routes-only` / `--api-key-*` are all **process-wide**, so a static key and a process cap set cannot represent a session. My earlier finding that `--a2a` publishes the 8 embedded `std/io` exports (`writeBytes`, `exit`, `readLine`, …) as callable A2A **skills** turned out to be the *less* severe half: `--routes-only` does suppress those, which also means the older upstream `#145` is genuinely FIXED and this is not its regression. Two further first-party facts for whoever builds this: MCP HTTP at `/mcp/` replies **SSE-framed** (`event: message` + `data:`), not a plain JSON body; and module resolution is **cwd-sensitive** — serving `design_docs/sketches/` from the repo root fails `LDR001: module not found: sketches/worldtypes` while serving `sketches/` from `design_docs/` succeeds, so the charter's 2026-07-23 live-test premise row (which records no cwd) is insufficient as launch configuration. **CONSEQUENCE: reuse paths (a) and (b) are REJECTED ON EVIDENCE, and the design takes path (c)** — request a narrow public serving seam from the existing `internal/apiserver` (mount MCP-HTTP/A2A handlers on a caller-owned mux; resolve principal/session before discovery *or* invocation; caller supplies the exact visible descriptors; named invocations route back with the same session; MCP tools and A2A skills generated from that one set; **no built-in tool unless the caller supplies it**; upstream keeps codec ownership and SSE conformance). Path (b) — a sidecar per session — was rejected because it makes process lifetime the session model and still exposes `submit_feedback`; reverse-proxy filtering was rejected because it would make World parse and re-encode MCP/A2A, the exact reinvention DESIGN.md §3.7 forbids. **ROUTED UPSTREAM ON BOTH CHANNELS: `sunholo-data/ailang#498`** + `msg_20260728_015014_8e5a281e`, with the full stdio repro, the version pin, `#145` cited as the fixed predecessor, a narrower interim ask (a flag that suppresses `submit_feedback` + document that `--caps` gates execution not discovery), and the **cause stated as a labelled HYPOTHESIS** since upstream source was never inspected. **THE QUORUM EARNED ITS KEEP TWICE AGAIN.** Round 1 (both present, `metered=$0.0634`) **BLOCKED**: `gpt5-6-sol` — no bounded-wait contract anywhere on the projection path (no timeout source, maximum, context-propagation rule, cleanup rule, error mapping, acceptance test or mutation for a stalled resolver/registry/broker/verifier/client; "broker unavailability returns an error" is not a contract); `gemini-3-1-pro` — mounting SSE-framed MCP handlers on the worldd daemon ignores that a REST daemon's `ReadTimeout`/`WriteTimeout` **abruptly kill long-lived SSE streams**. **I ran gemini's own "verify" step, and it collapsed its two-branch fix to ONE branch**: gemini offered "use `http.ResponseController` **or** document that the daemon lacks global timeouts", and the second branch is **FALSE here** — VERIFIED BY ME at `host/daemon/daemon.go:409-414` wiring constants at `:77-91`: `ReadHeaderTimeout` 5s / `ReadTimeout` 30s / `WriteTimeout` 30s / `IdleTimeout` 120s, **the D7 bound-constant block that `w-worldd-m2` A2 ratified and FROZE**. FROZEN CORE → no local patch; routed both channels as **`sunholo-data/ailang#493`** + `msg_20260727_183949_4951d6bc`. **A PRIOR FIRE WAS LOST**: the 16:12 run was killed by the driver's stall watchdog at 17:02 (rc=143) leaving **no artifacts** (verified: no worktree, no branch, no commit, no #9 comment, no log entry) — recorded so the numbering gap is not later read as a missing entry. **THE BLOCKING FINDING — an API that could not express a commit its own kernel accepts.** `POST /v1/commit` answered a genesis commit (zero observed head) with **400 `observedHead: hashref: empty hashref text`**, so the acceptance check "a genesis+commit episode driven **entirely** over REST" was unreachable. **Three parties found it independently**: the codex executor flagged the tension in its own final message and declined to invent a zero-ref encoding; the controller proved it with a first-party throwaway probe (embedded **accepts**, REST **400**) rather than forwarding the executor's claim; and the **sprint-evaluator BLOCKED round 1 (82/100)** having derived it separately, adding the cause nobody else named — the helper was called **`seedRESTGenesis` while calling `PutWorld`/`SelectHead` directly**, a misleading name that hid the gap from the executor's own review. **The fix, and its deliberate narrowness**: `parseGenesisRef` accepts `""` as the zero `HashRef` for **`observedHead` ONLY** — not a second encoding, since `HashRef.String()` **already** renders the zero value as `""`, so this round-trips the existing one. It **deliberately does NOT extend to `prevEntryHash`**: `store.Commit` **WRITES** a zero there that `store.GetLogEntry` **CANNOT READ BACK**, so accepting it would let a client append a log entry no reader can ever load — a worse defect than the one being closed. **The first draft of the fix DID include `prevEntryHash`; the new equivalence test failed against it, and that failure is what surfaced the M1 store asymmetry** (carried as CF-B-2, a kernel-side decision). **Controller's own independent evidence**: both gates green on the pinned v0.30.0 with `host/replay` **RUNNING 13.2s not SKIP**, `verify_ail.sh` at EXACTLY 4/4 identities / 9 modules / 14 tests, bench smoke rc=0, gofmt/vet clean, scope clean **by diff not by claim** (`host/store`, `world/`, `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `tools/`, `design_docs/`, `.github/` all byte-unchanged; no new deps, allowlist green); and **FIVE MUTATIONS, each RED then reverted GREEN** — clamp ceiling 500→1000 (RED "returned 510 items, want 500"); `MaxBytesReader` removed (RED at **400 not 413**, precisely the plan's predicted null case); the 409's machine-readable heads dropped **while keeping the prose message** (RED — proving a prose-only 409 is a dead end for a re-planning client); `observedHead` reverted to strict (RED, re-introduces the bug); the lenience over-widened to `prevEntryHash` (**RED at status 200** — it succeeds in writing the unreadable entry). **Evaluator round 2: PASS 89/100, ZERO blocking** — it re-derived the `prevEntryHash` asymmetry with its own probe rather than accepting the controller's account, cross-checked the N+1 arithmetic, and **caught a defect the CONTROLLER introduced in the fix**: the `parseGenesisRef` doc comment cited `TestGenesisRefLenienceIsExactlyTwoFields`, a test that does not exist after the fix narrowed from two fields to one. Fixed before merge — a comment naming a non-existent gate is how a future reader concludes there is no gate. **`bench/BASELINE.md` now covers the FULL surface with NO PENDING rows** (all six re-measured in ONE 200x invocation so no row comes from a different run than its neighbours): REST commit **0.5763 ms p95** (61×, a ~0.03 ms transport tax over the embedded floor — essentially all commit cost is the kernel's fsync); log range **1.248 ms** at limit=100 and **4.915 ms** at limit=500 — **3.94× the time for 5× the rows**, i.e. the deliberate N+1 is linear and 24× inside budget, so **no range-read store method is justified**, and the file now records what evidence would overturn that.
